## Final pass: the three-lens read (2026-09-26)

*The cold open, Acts One–Three and the tag, by the Ep1 writer, on one reader's three-lens review of the scene-craft draft (newcomer, insider, and "do we feel it"), plus its notes on documentation. **Why:** the same showrunner notes as the pass above, and the documentation note in full: "also as far as documentation, we want to be sure that we are leaving appropriate detail where someone could pick up where we left off". Inputs re-read at the start: the live [SHOWRUNNER-NOTES](../../production/SHOWRUNNER-NOTES.md) (a new note 1, the elevation-ideas menu, arrived during the pass and doesn't touch these segments), tone-and-dialogue's "Scene craft and film grammar" and R1–R21, [dialogue-craft](../../_sources/research/dialogue-craft.md), and [flow-and-continuity](../../bible/flow-and-continuity.md) §5a.*

*What was touched: only the cold open, Acts One–Three and the tag, this log (row 18, the counter, the cut list and this section) and [open-questions.md](open-questions.md). Every edit was a targeted replacement. **Act Four, its writer's notes, the header and the draft-2 appendix were not touched.** Nothing was committed. Nothing has been read aloud, recorded, heard or watched.*

**The reader's verdict, in one line:** the scene-craft pass works; four things were left: about seven lines that rang false or read unclearly, a new habit of Mas asking short questions so someone else can deliver the reveal, four places where a newcomer needs outside knowledge, and a Mas–Alyi bond that Act Four's turn depends on but the first half only lets us decode.

### Where this pass declined or bent a note, and why

- **N2's longer post is a lock option, not the default.** The reader gave the fuller wording from memory ("…created as an open source (which is why I named it "Open" AI), non-profit company…"). facts.md logs only the fragment, as "part of a longer 1:36am post", and nothing was fetched in this pass, so the verified fragment stays on screen until the facts owner fetches the post (open question 65). The bio line, which needs no fetch, is taken now. Whether "Open" may stay unswapped inside a real quote goes to the naming owner (open question 66).
- **N4: the plate carries the question, instead of the reader's fallback of a readable `BILLS: 0`.** A readable `BILLS: 0` would add a show-voice interruption, and "nothing passed" is the joke Ep2's `9 FORUMS · … · 0 BILLS` card already tells. Replacing the plate that repeated the line gives the newcomer what the hands were for at no extra interruption. `BILLS: 0` stays a zero-read egg, and the facts owner confirms the plate's paraphrase (open question 67).
- **N5 gained a visible trigger the reader didn't ask for.** "it's 2023, by the way." with nothing to prompt it would be a blurt (R19), so Sydney's bubble carries a `2022` date stamp.
- **F22: "as he taps it" became "a beat after the tap".** The reader asked to keep the tap-then-say that Act Four's S1.11–S1.12 rhymes; saying it on the tap would change the order.
- **The sc 12 bond beat isn't applied.** The reader's cheapest option (Mas looks up for once, and Alyi's reflection looks away first) puts a Mas look on an act-out at a real event, and the reader flagged it for the POV and guardrails owners rather than recommending it. It's open question 68. The bond now rests on N1 and the direction that lets launch night's exchange play as private.

# Revision log (dialogue pass 5, 2026-09-26)

*The cold open, Acts One–Three and the tag, by the Ep1 writer. **Why:** after the scene-craft pass was committed (`890fc82`), a workflow resume re-ran three critics on these segments: a naturalness read, a newcomer-and-insider read and a facts-and-guardrails read. Their findings are kept in [the critiques file](../../production/reviews/2026-09-26-dialogue-pass-5-critiques.md). The showrunner's standing notes governed every call: let conversations play out (note 7), scenes to be felt (note 4), clear to a newcomer without lectures (notes 8 and 9), guidelines, not hard rules (note 12), and firm guardrails.*

***Finished after an interrupted run.** A first run applied most of the fixes before the lead stopped it mid-edit at about 22:33. This finishing pass re-read the showrunner notes and the critiques, compared the script with its committed version (`git -C /home/jgon/project/art/mrmas diff 890fc82 -- show/episodes/ep01/script.md`), checked every half-applied edit, applied or declined what was left, and brought [open-questions.md](open-questions.md) up to date. The first run hadn't reached that file: the script already cited open questions 69 and 70, which didn't exist. The "Run" column below says which run made each change.*

*What was touched: the cold open, Acts One–Three and the tag; in the scene-craft log, cut-list item 7 and the counter's table of drafts; this log; and open-questions.md. Every edit was a targeted replacement. **Act Four, its writer's notes, the header, §1 of the writer's notes and the draft-2 appendix were not touched.** The header and §1 now lag by ≈ 13.5 s (see the handoffs). Nothing was committed. **Nothing in this pass has been read aloud, recorded, heard or watched:** every length is an estimate from word counts.*

## Where this pass declined or bent a note, and why (dialogue pass 5)

- **Sc 17, the footnote (bent).** The naturalness read's rewrite, "So I'm adding a footnote. What it costs depends on who else is buying.", keeps the half the insider read flagged: "who else is buying" tells insiders the register's punchline before it rolls in. Mario's first line, "It doesn't say what it costs.", is setup enough. "A short one." keeps his turn at two sentences and makes a promise his next line breaks ("It has an appendix."), which is his runner.
- **Sc 7, Rima (bent).** The read's "Mas… a million people. That's for them, isn't it?" puts dots after the name, which a stock voice stalls on as surely as a full stop. The name moves to the end of the phrase instead, the read's own general fix.
- **Two quip volleys stay** (the newcomer-and-insider read's note to the naturalness read; the read itself says none of them drags):
  - "I have one concern." / "Just one?" / "It has sub-concerns. I've grouped them by how worried we should be." Mario's answer is already two sentences, and the exchange is settled across episodes: Ep3 calls back "sub-concerns" (open question 51).
  - "which one am i?" / "Most of them." Sucram's opening to Mas runs three sentences, and "Most of them." is the button on it: motivated seasoning, which note 7 allows. A longer answer would explain the joke.
  - The third ("Longer than that.") is fixed, above.
- **Lines repeated across episodes stay in Ep1** ("Last one…", "Don't mind me.", Radnus's congratulations and "thank you.", "That's how a race starts."). Ep1 plays first, so the later episode changes, and both already have. Ep2's reporter says "One more and we'll let you get back on the trail…", its Humanist "I'm just moving in downstairs. I hope that's all right.", and its Radnus "Lovely garden. I see they let your chatbot in." / "as a guest.". Ep3's Maon no longer says "That's how you get two.".
- **The unseen questioners (across 3):** Ep1's panel host keeps "Last one, Mas. What's the best part of the job?". Ep2 has changed its reporter's rhythm, so the two no longer match.
- **Newcomer read, "little on-screen threat" in Acts One–Three:** no change on the page. It isn't a missing fact, and the no-spoiler rule limits what can come before Act Four. It goes on the reel's list (open question 47).
- **Newcomer read, the lit window:** Ep1 raises the question on purpose, and the answer is Ep3's and the season's (open question 49).
- **Newcomer read, `MODEL WEIGHTS` on Kram's crate:** no change. The read says the wet paint carries the joke.
- **Facts read, the stale rows in facts.md and the research, and the season-wide ruling on voicing [H] words:** not this pass's files. Ep1 already keeps its [H] words in print (open question 53). The corrections are listed for the facts owner in open question 74.
- **The header and §1's segment rows** are outside these segments. The figures for them are in the handoffs.
- **Radnus's pace in the counter** stays at 135 wpm, so this log's figures compare with the earlier ones. If his quicker direction is cast (open question 73), his ≈ 55 words play about 4 s faster than counted.

# Appendix: Act Four draft 2 (superseded)

*Kept for reference. Draft 3 (the POV pass, above) replaces it. The text is verbatim except that its headings are demoted one level, so the file's anchors stay unique. The Act Four production boards (`production/act4/`) and the recorded takes in `audio/ep01/act4/dialogue/` were built from this version.*

### ACT FOUR · THE BLIP, TOLD TWICE

*Movement 4 (the pilot's sanctioned long movement) · 12:31–19:45 · meters: the firing tally (mark 3), hearts, the badge, the lobby sign. The internal act-out is the `WHAT THEY DIDN'T KNOW` card.*

#### 24. INT. LAS VEGAS HOTEL SUITE — DAY [BASE] · I · 12:31–12:36 (2 bars)

`RAIL: NOV 17, 2023 · ~NOON PT · LAS VEGAS`

A high suite over the Strip on the race weekend. Below the window, a crane truck grinds along the closed street circuit. As it passes, every glass in the suite shivers: a flute on the minibar, a tumbler, the ice bucket, a vase. Mas sits at the desk with his laptop and his glass. His glass does not shiver.

On the laptop: `BOARD · VIDEO CALL · JOIN`. His cursor rests on JOIN.

Before he clicks, the frame turns to blueprint:

#### 25. THE PLAN [BLUEPRINT] · P · 12:36–13:21 (18 bars: 3 · 7 · 5 · 3)

*Cyan #7FDBFF on navy #0B1E3F, on a drafting-table grid. Everything explained here is accurate; the plan failing is the joke.*

**THE WORD (3 bars).** Stamped across the grid:

`HOW TO FIRE A CEO WHO OWNS NOTHING.`

**THE DIAGRAM (7 bars).** Linework draws itself in whole-pixel strokes, one label per beat.
- Nine blueprint chair outlines sit on the grid, each with a nameplate. MUSIC: a chip-voiced waltz, the knee motif in three-four, played like a party game. On the beat, three outlines stand up on small legs and walk politely off the grid: `DIRE`, then `NOVIHS`, then `DRUH` (the last wearing a campaign sticker with no logo and no date). Where they stood, a stamp: `LEFT EARLIER IN 2023` [V/K: three 2023 departures; exit dates not shown]. The waltz stops. (Keep NOVIHS name-only, away from any NOLE cue: no lamp click, no zAI colours.)
- Six outlines remain: `MAS`, `GERG`, `ALYI`, `NELEH`, `MADA`, `THE QUIET VOTE`. Four are circled: `THESE FOUR VOTE`. (Egg: MADA's outline has bolts drawn at its feet.)
- A box draws itself around the six: `NOPEAI · THE NONPROFIT`. A thick arrow labelled `CONTROLS` points down into a bigger box: `NOPEAI · THE COMPANY (CAPPED PROFIT)`.
- A banner runs across the top box: `THE BOARD'S DUTY: THE MISSION. NOT THE INVESTORS.`
- Outside a drawn fence sits a key ring the size of a steering wheel: `MACROSOFT · ~$10B IN (REPORTED) · VOTES: 0`.
- Inside the company box is one small box, `CEO`, stamped underneath: `EQUITY: 0 (HIS TESTIMONY)` [V]. In the equity box, a tiny moth drawn in blueprint linework opens its wings.

**THE PLAN (5 bars).** Four little blueprint figures walk onto the grid: a door outline (ALYI), a figure holding a glowing paper (NELEH), a figure with a loading spinner over its head (MADA) and a black square (THE QUIET VOTE). A numbered path appears under their feet:

`1. NOON · VIDEO CALL ✓`
`2. BLOG POST ✓`
`3. INTERIM CEO ✓`
`4. ______________`

The figures tick off 1, 2 and 3 in stride. They reach 4 and stop. The blank line is just a line.

**NELEH** *(blueprint, precise)*\
Step four.

**MADA** *(blueprint)*\
Good question.

**THE BREAK (3 bars).** A chalk stroke starts to fill the blank, squeaks and snaps. The corner of the blueprint curls up, and Las Vegas neon bleeds through behind it. The blueprint tears along the line of step 4, straight back into the suite we already know. His cursor is still on JOIN. He clicks.
